<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We solve the probability-preserving form of Banach's simple Lebesgue-spectrum problem in smooth dynamics. We construct a <i>C</i><sup>∞</sup> diffeomorphism of the three-torus that preserves standard volume and whose Koopman operator has simple Lebesgue spectrum on the entire mean-zero <i>L</i><sup>2</sup> space.

- **claim-main-simple-lebesgue** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exist a $C^\infty$ volume-preserving diffeomorphism $T\colon\mathbb T^3\to\mathbb T^3$ and a real function $f\in L^2_0(\mathbb T^3,\mu)$ such that $$\{f\circ T^n:n\in\mathbb Z\}$$ is an orthonormal basis of $L^2_0(\mathbb T^3,\mu)$. Consequently $T$ is ergodic, and $U_T$ on this space is unitarily equivalent to multiplication by the coordinate $w$ on $L^2(S^1,m)$.
  - Explanation: A smooth volume-preserving diffeomorphism of the three-torus exists whose Koopman orbit of one real function is an orthonormal basis of the whole mean-zero square-integrable space, so the map is ergodic with simple Lebesgue spectrum there.
- **claim-passage-coordinate-change** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-passage)): Fix <a href="#eq:twist" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:twist">[eq:twist]</a>, its physical volume-preserving coordinate chart, and a finite disjoint packet list. Choose a positive integer $c$ so large that the compact ranges $m(c+\phi(\mathbb T))$, for the distinct old indices $m$, are pairwise disjoint and avoid zero. Set $V=c+\phi$. Fix $J\in C^\infty(\mathbb T;\mathbb R)$ with $J'>0$ on an open arc $I'$ and fix $0\le\psi\le1$ in $C_c^\infty(I')$. For integers $N\to\infty$, choose $\Delta_N$ so that $$\label{eq:passage-rounding} \|\Delta_N\|\le N^{-1},\qquad k_N:=N\left(\int_\mathbb TV(u)\,\mathrm du+\Delta_N\right)\in\mathbb Z, \qquad H_N'=N(V+\Delta_N).$$ Put $\sigma_N(a)=N^{-1}+N^{-2}J(a)$. Then $$\label{eq:passage-map} T_N(\theta,u,a)= \bigl(\theta+H_N(u+\sigma_N(a))-H_N(u),\, u+\sigma_N(a),\,a\bigr)$$ is a volume-preserving smooth torus diffeomorphism, and both $T_N$ and $T_N^{-1}$ converge smoothly to $S$ and $S^{-1}$, also in the fixed physical coordinates. In the new coordinates $$\label{eq:passage-chart} (\theta',u',a')=C_N(\theta,u,a) =(u,a,\theta-H_N(u)),$$ it is the twist of speed $\sigma_N$ and good arc $I'$.* *Here are its packet and density properties. For an old packet indexed by $\alpha$, with index $m_\alpha$ and amplitude $A_\alpha$, define $$\label{eq:stationary-image} E_\alpha=\{(m_\alpha V(u),a):(u,a)\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}A_\alpha\}.$$ One may choose pairwise disjoint, arbitrarily small fixed neighborhoods of these compact sets, and smooth cutoffs $\chi_\alpha(s,a)$ supported in those neighborhoods and equal to one near $E_\alpha$. Retain the finitely many new packets $$\begin{aligned} G_{\alpha,j}(\theta',u',a') &=\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(j\theta')\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(m_\alpha a') \psi(u')\chi_\alpha(j/N,u')C_{\alpha,j}(u'), \label{eq:retained-packets}\\ C_{\alpha,j}(a) &=\int_\mathbb TA_\alpha(u,a)\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(m_\alpha H_N(u)-ju)\,\mathrm du. \label{eq:exact-passage-coefficient} \end{aligned}$$ All their angular indices are nonzero for sufficiently large $N$. For each $\alpha$, $$\label{eq:transport-norm} \left\\|\sum_jG_{\alpha,j}\circ C_N -\psi(a)A_\alpha(u,a)\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(m_\alpha\theta)\right\\|_2=o(1).$$ They are disjoint as packets, and their moduli are independent of the new spare coordinate $a'$. Every constant attached to an old packet is preserved by this linear transport and these cutoffs.* *For a single old packet write, with zero extension outside its branch, $$\label{eq:W-definition} W(s,a)=\frac{\|A(u,a)\|^2}{\|m\phi'(u)\|},\qquad s=mV(u).$$ Let $K(s,y)$ be the density of the pushforward of $W(s,a)\psi(a)^2\,\mathrm da$ under $a\mapsto sJ(a)$. It is continuous and compactly supported in $(s,y)$, with its $s$ support away from zero. For any fixed positive integer $P$ and residue $h\pmod P$, the unfolded temporal density of the retained packets with $j\equiv h\pmod P$ differs in $L^1(\mathbb R)$ by $o(1)$ from $$\label{eq:fine-density-unfolded} Q_{N,h}(t)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z}K(t,Nt-h-kP).$$ Consequently their density on $\mathbb T$ differs in $L^1(\mathbb T)$ by $o(1)$ from $\sum_{l\in\mathbb Z}Q_{N,h}(z+l)$. The assertions hold after summing over any of the finitely many colors. All $o(1)$ terms refer to the old data, $c,J,\psi,P$, and support neighborhoods fixed before $N$ tends to infinity. In particular, $N$ may be required to exceed any prescribed bound.
  - Explanation: A slow passage replaces an integrable twist by a nearby twist in new coordinates and transports packets. Stationary phase locates the new angular indices and gives the fine-scale spectral density.
- **claim-fast-variable-averaging** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fast-average)): Let $P>0$, and let $G(t,y)$ be continuous, compactly supported in $t$, and $P$-periodic in $y$. Then, uniformly in real offsets $\beta_N$, $$\label{eq:fast-average} \int_\mathbb RG(t,Nt+\beta_N)\,\mathrm dt \longrightarrow \frac1P\int_\mathbb R\int_0^P G(t,y)\,\mathrm dy\,\mathrm dt.$$ In particular, suppose $w\ge0$ is continuous and compactly supported in a compact set $K\subset\mathbb R\setminus\{0\}$. For continuous integrable densities $\rho,\widetilde\rho$ define $$\mathcal P_{s,P}\rho(y) =\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z}\|s\|^{-1}\rho((y+kP)/s).$$ Assume these periodizations are jointly continuous for $s\in K$. Then $$\begin{aligned} \label{eq:law-diagonal-comparison} &\lim_{N\to\infty} \int_\mathbb Rw(s) \|\mathcal P_{s,P}\rho(Ns+\beta_N) -\mathcal P_{s,P}\widetilde\rho(Ns+\beta_N)\|\,\mathrm ds\\ &\hspace{12mm}= \frac1P\int_\mathbb Rw(s)\int_0^P \|\mathcal P_{s,P}\rho(y)-\mathcal P_{s,P}\widetilde\rho(y)\| \,\mathrm dy\,\mathrm ds \le\frac{\\|w\\|_1}{P}\\|\rho-\widetilde\rho\\|_1.\notag \end{aligned}$$ Joint continuity is automatic, for example, if the densities are continuous with bounds $C/(1+y^2)$, or are continuous and compactly supported. Neither density need have mass one.
  - Explanation: Integrals of a slowly varying, fast-oscillating periodic function converge to its period average, uniformly in the offset. This lets periodized jitter laws be compared along the fast variable.
- **claim-jitter-law-realization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-law-approx)): For any strictly positive smooth probability density $\rho$ on $\mathbb R$ and any $\varepsilon>0$, there are a smooth real circle function $J$, an open arc $I'$ with $\|\mathbb T\setminus I'\|<\varepsilon$ and $J'>0$ on $I'$, and $0\le\psi\le1$ in $C_c^\infty(I')$, such that $$J_*(\psi^2\,\mathrm da)=\widetilde\rho(y)\,\mathrm dy, \qquad \widetilde\rho\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R), \qquad \\|\rho-\widetilde\rho\\|_1<\varepsilon.$$ Here the cutoff law is a subprobability measure; no renormalization is made. Also $\int_\mathbb T(1-\psi^2)\,\mathrm da<\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: Every strictly positive smooth probability density is approximated in total variation by the pushforward of a cutoff measure under a smooth circle function that is increasing on a good arc with small complement.
- **claim-weighted-neutral-refresh** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-neutral)): Given a twist, its fixed physical chart, a finite disjoint smooth packet list with color densities $p_r$, a positive integer $P$, and a positive tolerance, one can perform a passage with the following simultaneous properties. Its physical map and inverse lie in any prescribed smooth neighborhood of the old ones. Its new good-arc complement is arbitrarily small. Every color vector is transported within the prescribed $L^2$ tolerance, retaining constant tags and disjointness. The new packet moduli are independent of the new spare coordinate. The output densities $p'_r$ and the densities $p'_{r,h}$ contributed by output indices $j\equiv h\pmod P$ satisfy $$\label{eq:neutral-densities} \\|p'_r-p_r\\|_1<\varepsilon, \qquad \\|p'_{r,h}-p_r/P\\|_1<\varepsilon \quad(0\le h<P),$$ where $\varepsilon>0$ may be prescribed arbitrarily. The integer $c$ may first be required to exceed any fixed bound, and the retained supports may use any sufficiently small fixed enlargements of <a href="#eq:stationary-image" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stationary-image">[eq:stationary-image]</a>. If colors were disjoint in their old angular indices alone, their output angular index sets are still disjoint. The final passage integer $N$ may exceed any prescribed bound.
  - Explanation: A neutral passage transports colored packets within tolerance and spreads each color density evenly over residue classes. Maps stay close to the old ones and the good arc complement stays small.
- **claim-finite-family-passages** (construction, [source](#stmt-rem-finite-family-passages)): A common passage can handle finitely many packet lists, requiring disjointness only within each list. Choose $c$ using the union of their old indices, choose the neutral cutoff and slope to meet all their finitely many mass and density bounds, and choose $N$ above all the resulting lower bounds. Support cutoffs are chosen separately for each list, disjoint within that list. The norm and density conclusions then hold for every list; no orthogonality between different lists is asserted.
  - Explanation: One passage can serve several packet lists at once, with disjointness needed only inside each list and no orthogonality between lists.
- **claim-index-signal-transmission** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-signal)): For the fixed data $b,D_0,d$ there are an integer $P\ge2$ and a number $\gamma_0>0$ with the following property. Any fixed $0<\gamma\le\gamma_0$ can be used, independently of $B$.* *Let $S$ be an intermediate map, and let $$F_r=\sum_{m\in\mathcal M_r} A_{r,m}(u,a)\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(m\theta)$$ be finite smooth packet data in its good arc. Suppose that the finite sets $\mathcal M_r\subset\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}$ are mutually disjoint, $\|A_{r,m}(u,a)\|$ is independent of $a$, and $b\le \|m\|/B\le D_0$. Write $p_{r,m}$ for the spectral density of the indicated packet. Given any $\varepsilon,\delta_x,\delta_z>0$ and prescribed positive bounds for the physical $L^2$ transport errors, a passage produces finite output packet sums $F_{r,m}^+$, approximating the corresponding input packets in physical $L^2$, whose spectral densities satisfy $$\sum_{r,m}\left\\|p_{r,m}^+ -p_{r,m}(z)\left[1+2\gamma\mathbf 1_{\gcd(m,P)=1} \mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\{d(m/B)\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(-Nz)\}\right]\right\\|_{L^1(\mathbb T)} <\varepsilon. \label{eq:signal-conclusion}$$ The new scale is $B^+=NcB$, and the new speed is $\sigma_N$ from Lemma <a href="#lem:passage" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:passage">2</a>. For every point $(u',a')$ in the coefficient support of a retained output packet of index $j$ descending from $(r,m)$, there is $(u,a)\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}A_{r,m}$ such that $$\left\|\frac{j}{B^+}-\frac mB\right\|<\delta_x, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_\mathbb T\bigl(j\sigma_N(u'),m\phi(u)\bigr)<\delta_z. \label{eq:signal-support-closeness}$$ Its modulus is independent of the new spare coordinate. Distinct old angular indices have disjoint output angular-index supports. Conjugate symmetry is preserved when present.* *The passage and its inverse can lie in any prescribed smooth neighborhood of $S$ and $S^{-1}$ in physical coordinates, and the new good arc can have arbitrarily small complement. The choices can be made in the order $c$, a bounded smooth jitter and cutoffs, and finally the integer $N$. After the preceding choices, every sufficiently large integer $N$ works. In particular an arbitrary lower bound on $N$ can be imposed.
  - Explanation: A positive Fourier law and a finite prime sieve give a modulus and a strength limit so that a passage modulates each packet density by a small sinusoid whose coefficient follows the normalized index, for indices coprime to the modulus.
- **claim-refresh-and-transmit** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-refresh-transmit)): Fix $b,D_0,d,P,\gamma$ as in Lemma <a href="#lem:signal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:signal">7</a>, and put $$\lambda=\gamma\frac{\|(\mathbb Z/P\mathbb Z)^\times\|}{P}>0. \label{eq:signal-lambda}$$ Suppose finite colored packet data have mutually disjoint angular-index sets and moduli independent of the spare coordinate. Suppose their normalized indices lie in a fixed compact subset of $\{x:b<\|x\|<D_0\}$. Let $p_r$ be the color densities, and let $d_r:\mathbb T\to\mathbb C$ be continuous functions such that on the support of every input packet of color $r$, $$\|d(m/B)-d_r(m\phi(u)\bmod1)\|\le\omega. \label{eq:signal-color-match}$$ Then a neutral passage followed by a transmission gives output colors with $$\sum_r\left\\|p_r^+ -p_r(z)\bigl[1+2\lambda\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\{d_r(z)\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(-Nz)\}\bigr] \right\\|_1 \le 2\gamma\omega\sum_r\\|p_r\\|_1+\varepsilon, \label{eq:signal-refreshed}$$ where $\varepsilon>0$ is arbitrary and $N$ is the second passage integer. The physical map and inverse, the physical packet vectors, and the normalized-index and temporal-frequency displacements can all be controlled with arbitrary prescribed positive error bounds. Output packet moduli remain independent of the spare coordinate, and colors remain disjoint in angular indices. The final $N$ can exceed any prescribed bound. Conjugate-symmetric data can be treated conjugate-symmetrically.* *If different colors start in separated compact normalized-index sets, any fixed sufficiently small enlargements of those sets can contain their outputs. The same conclusion holds through any fixed finite number of such pairs, with positive margins around the band $b<\|x\|<D_0$. If the input supports satisfy $\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_\mathbb T(m/B,m\phi(u))\le\kappa$, the output supports satisfy the same inequality with $\kappa$ increased by an arbitrarily small amount.
  - Explanation: Placing a neutral passage before transmission removes the coprimality restriction at the cost of a fixed factor in the signal strength. Colors receive a modulation given by a continuous function with controlled error.
- **claim-tagged-packet-prediction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-prediction)): Let $S$ be an intermediate map, expressed in a fixed physical volume-preserving coordinate chart. Let $$F_r=\sum_{m\in\mathcal M_r} A_{r,m}(u,a)\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(m\theta), \qquad F=\sum_r F_r,$$ be a finite list of colors of smooth packets supported compactly in the good arc. Suppose that the finite sets $\mathcal M_r\subset\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}$ are pairwise disjoint, that $\|A_{r,m}(u,a)\|$ is independent of $a$, and that the list is invariant under complex conjugation: $F_{r^*}=\overline{F_r}$. In particular, $F$ is real. Let $p_r$ be the corresponding spectral densities, put $p=\sum_r p_r$, and suppose that $$\int_\mathbb Tp(z)\,\,\mathrm dz\le \frac32.$$ Assign constants $b_r\in\mathbb C$ satisfying $b_{r^*}=\overline{b_r}$.* *Given $\varepsilon,\tau,\kappa>0$ and any neighborhood of the physical map and its inverse in the smooth topology, there are a finite sequence of passages, a final intermediate map $S'$, final packets $F'_r$, and a smooth function $\Psi:\mathbb T\to\mathbb C$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \sum_r\\|F'_r-F_r\\|_2&<\varepsilon, \label{eq:prediction-transport}\\ \int_\mathbb T\sqrt{p'(z)}\,\,\mathrm dz &\ge \int_\mathbb T\sqrt{p(z)}\,\,\mathrm dz-\tau, \qquad p'=\sum_r p'_r, \label{eq:prediction-hellinger}\\ \left\\|\sum_r b_rF'_r- \Psi(U_{S'})\sum_r F'_r\right\\|_2&<\varepsilon. \label{eq:prediction-error} \end{aligned}$$ Here the differences in <a href="#eq:prediction-transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prediction-transport">[eq:prediction-transport]</a> are measured in the original physical $L^2$ space. All the physical maps and their inverses can be kept in the prescribed neighborhood. The final good arc $I'$ satisfies $\|\mathbb T\setminus I'\|<\kappa$. The output colors again have disjoint nonzero angular-index sets, moduli independent of the spare coordinate, and conjugation symmetry. The multiplier can be chosen with $\Psi(-z)=\overline{\Psi(z)}$.
  - Explanation: Finitely many passages produce a spectral multiplier that predicts the constant tags of colored packets in physical square norm, while the square-root integral of the total density loses arbitrarily little.
- **claim-finite-phase-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-phase-tests)): Fix finitely many expressions $$P_j(z,w)=\sum_{\nu\in\mathcal E_j}c_{j,\nu}(z)w^\nu, \qquad c_{j,\nu}\in L^1(\mathbb T),$$ where each $\mathcal E_j$ is finite. For every $\eta>0$, successively large positive integers $L_1,\ldots,L_n$ can be chosen so that, simultaneously for all $j$, $$\left\|\int_\mathbb TP_j\bigl(z,\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(L_1z),\ldots,\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(L_nz)\bigr)\,\,\mathrm dz -\int_\mathbb T\int_{\mathbb T^n}P_j\bigl(z,\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(y_1),\ldots,\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(y_n)\bigr) \,\,\mathrm dy\,\,\mathrm dz\right\|<\eta. \label{eq:phase-tests}$$ At each selection, $L_i$ may additionally be required to exceed any finite lower bound determined by the preceding choices and the then-current construction data.
  - Explanation: Choosing successively large integer frequencies makes the diagonal average of finitely many Laurent expressions with integrable coefficients agree with the product-torus average up to any tolerance.
- **claim-flat-vector** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-flat-vector)): For every intermediate twist and every good arc $I$, there is a real centered vector $v$ of spectral density $1$, using two nonzero angular indices and coefficients supported in a compact subinterval of $I$.
  - Explanation: On any good arc there is an explicit real centered vector built from two opposite angular modes whose spectral density is exactly one.
- **claim-one-stage** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-stage)): Let $(S,f)$ be admissible, let $h$ be a real smooth function in physical coordinates with $\int h=0$ and $\\|h\\|_\infty\le1$, and let $\eta,\rho,\tau>0$. In every prescribed neighborhood of $S$ there is an admissible pair $(S',f')$, with good arc $I'$, and a finite Laurent polynomial $P$ such that $$\label{eq:stage-conclusion} \\|f'-f\\|_2<\tau,\qquad \|\mathbb T\setminus I'\|<\rho,\qquad \\|h-P(U_{S'})f'\\|_2 <\|\mathbb T\setminus I\|^{1/2}+\eta.$$
  - Explanation: One inductive stage keeps the flat vector close, shrinks the bad arc and approximates a prescribed bounded centered observable by a Laurent polynomial in the Koopman operator applied to the new vector.
- **claim-dynamical-profile** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-dynamical-profile)): For the diffeomorphism $T$ and normalized standard volume $\mu$ of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, every integer $k\ge2$ and every fixed choice of measurable sets $A_1,\ldots,A_k\subseteq\mathbb T^3$ satisfy $$\mu\left(\bigcap_{i=1}^k T^{-t_i}A_i\right) \longrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^k\mu(A_i)$$ whenever $t_1<\cdots<t_k$ are integers and $\min_{1\le i<k}(t_{i+1}-t_i)\to\infty$. Moreover, the Kolmogorov–Sinai entropy satisfies $h_\mu(T)=0$, and all three Lyapunov exponents of $T$, counted with multiplicity, vanish at $\mu$-almost every point.
  - Explanation: The constructed map is mixing of every order, has zero Kolmogorov–Sinai entropy and all its Lyapunov exponents vanish almost everywhere.

        "exact_quote": "Fix <a href=\"#eq:twist\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:twist\">[eq:twist]</a>, its physical volume-preserving coordinate chart, and a finite disjoint packet list. Choose a positive integer $c$ so large that the compact ranges $m(c+\\phi(\\mathbb T))$, for the distinct old indices $m$, are pairwise disjoint and avoid zero. Set $V=c+\\phi$. Fix $J\\in C^\\infty(\\mathbb T;\\mathbb R)$ with $J'>0$ on an open arc $I'$ and fix $0\\le\\psi\\le1$ in $C_c^\\infty(I')$. For integers $N\\to\\infty$, choose $\\Delta_N$ so that $$\\label{eq:passage-rounding}\n |\\Delta_N|\\le N^{-1},\\qquad\n k_N:=N\\left(\\int_\\mathbb TV(u)\\,\\mathrm du+\\Delta_N\\right)\\in\\mathbb Z,\n \\qquad H_N'=N(V+\\Delta_N).$$ Put $\\sigma_N(a)=N^{-1}+N^{-2}J(a)$. Then $$\\label{eq:passage-map}\n T_N(\\theta,u,a)=\n \\bigl(\\theta+H_N(u+\\sigma_N(a))-H_N(u),\\,\n u+\\sigma_N(a),\\,a\\bigr)$$ is a volume-preserving smooth torus diffeomorphism, and both $T_N$ and $T_N^{-1}$ converge smoothly to $S$ and $S^{-1}$, also in the fixed physical coordinates. In the new coordinates $$\\label{eq:passage-chart}\n (\\theta',u',a')=C_N(\\theta,u,a)\n             =(u,a,\\theta-H_N(u)),$$ it is the twist of speed $\\sigma_N$ and good arc $I'$.*\n\n\n\n\n\n*Here are its packet and density properties. For an old packet indexed by $\\alpha$, with index $m_\\alpha$ and amplitude $A_\\alpha$, define $$\\label{eq:stationary-image}\n E_\\alpha=\\{(m_\\alpha V(u),a):(u,a)\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}A_\\alpha\\}.$$ One may choose pairwise disjoint, arbitrarily small fixed neighborhoods of these compact sets, and smooth cutoffs $\\chi_\\alpha(s,a)$ supported in those neighborhoods and equal to one near $E_\\alpha$. Retain the finitely many new packets $$\\begin{aligned}\n G_{\\alpha,j}(\\theta',u',a')\n   &=\\mathop{\\mathrm{e}}(j\\theta')\\mathop{\\mathrm{e}}(m_\\alpha a')\n       \\psi(u')\\chi_\\alpha(j/N,u')C_{\\alpha,j}(u'),\n       \\label{eq:retained-packets}\\\\\n C_{\\alpha,j}(a)\n   &=\\int_\\mathbb TA_\\alpha(u,a)\\mathop{\\mathrm{e}}(m_\\alpha H_N(u)-ju)\\,\\mathrm du.\n       \\label{eq:exact-passage-coefficient}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All their angular indices are nonzero for sufficiently large $N$. For each $\\alpha$, $$\\label{eq:transport-norm}\n \\left\\|\\sum_jG_{\\alpha,j}\\circ C_N\n       -\\psi(a)A_\\alpha(u,a)\\mathop{\\mathrm{e}}(m_\\alpha\\theta)\\right\\|_2=o(1).$$ They are disjoint as packets, and their moduli are independent of the new spare coordinate $a'$. Every constant attached to an old packet is preserved by this linear transport and these cutoffs.*\n\n\n\n*For a single old packet write, with zero extension outside its branch, $$\\label{eq:W-definition}\n W(s,a)=\\frac{|A(u,a)|^2}{|m\\phi'(u)|},\\qquad s=mV(u).$$ Let $K(s,y)$ be the density of the pushforward of $W(s,a)\\psi(a)^2\\,\\mathrm da$ under $a\\mapsto sJ(a)$. It is continuous and compactly supported in $(s,y)$, with its $s$ support away from zero. For any fixed positive integer $P$ and residue $h\\pmod P$, the unfolded temporal density of the retained packets with $j\\equiv h\\pmod P$ differs in $L^1(\\mathbb R)$ by $o(1)$ from $$\\label{eq:fine-density-unfolded}\n Q_{N,h}(t)=\\sum_{k\\in\\mathbb Z}K(t,Nt-h-kP).$$ Consequently their density on $\\mathbb T$ differs in $L^1(\\mathbb T)$ by $o(1)$ from $\\sum_{l\\in\\mathbb Z}Q_{N,h}(z+l)$. The assertions hold after summing over any of the finitely many colors. All $o(1)$ terms refer to the old data, $c,J,\\psi,P$, and support neighborhoods fixed before $N$ tends to infinity. In particular, $N$ may be required to exceed any prescribed bound.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $P>0$, and let $G(t,y)$ be continuous, compactly supported in $t$, and $P$-periodic in $y$. For continuous integrable densities $\\rho,\\widetilde\\rho$ define $$\\mathcal P_{s,P}\\rho(y)\n     =\\sum_{k\\in\\mathbb Z}|s|^{-1}\\rho((y+kP)/s).$$ Assume these periodizations are jointly continuous for $s\\in K$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{eq:law-diagonal-comparison}\n &\\lim_{N\\to\\infty}\n \\int_\\mathbb Rw(s)\n |\\mathcal P_{s,P}\\rho(Ns+\\beta_N)\n       -\\mathcal P_{s,P}\\widetilde\\rho(Ns+\\beta_N)|\\,\\mathrm ds\\\\\n &\\hspace{12mm}=\n \\frac1P\\int_\\mathbb Rw(s)\\int_0^P\n |\\mathcal P_{s,P}\\rho(y)-\\mathcal P_{s,P}\\widetilde\\rho(y)|\n       \\,\\mathrm dy\\,\\mathrm ds\n \\le\\frac{\\|w\\|_1}{P}\\|\\rho-\\widetilde\\rho\\|_1.\\notag\n\\end{aligned}$$ Joint continuity is automatic, for example, if the densities are continuous with bounds $C/(1+y^2)$, or are continuous and compactly supported. Neither density need have mass one.",

        "exact_quote": "For any strictly positive smooth probability density $\\rho$ on $\\mathbb R$ and any $\\varepsilon>0$, there are a smooth real circle function $J$, an open arc $I'$ with $|\\mathbb T\\setminus I'|<\\varepsilon$ and $J'>0$ on $I'$, and $0\\le\\psi\\le1$ in $C_c^\\infty(I')$, such that $$J_*(\\psi^2\\,\\mathrm da)=\\widetilde\\rho(y)\\,\\mathrm dy,\n \\qquad \\widetilde\\rho\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R),\n \\qquad \\|\\rho-\\widetilde\\rho\\|_1<\\varepsilon.$$ Here the cutoff law is a subprobability measure; no renormalization is made. Also $\\int_\\mathbb T(1-\\psi^2)\\,\\mathrm da<\\varepsilon$.",

      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-law-approx",
          "exact_quote": "For any strictly positive smooth probability density $\\rho$ on $\\mathbb R$ and any $\\varepsilon>0$, there are a smooth real circle function $J$, an open arc $I'$ with $|\\mathbb T\\setminus I'|<\\varepsilon$ and $J'>0$ on $I'$, and $0\\le\\psi\\le1$ in $C_c^\\infty(I')$, such that $$J_*(\\psi^2\\,\\mathrm da)=\\widetilde\\rho(y)\\,\\mathrm dy,\n \\qquad \\widetilde\\rho\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R),\n \\qquad \\|\\rho-\\widetilde\\rho\\|_1<\\varepsilon.$$ Here the cutoff law is a subprobability measure; no renormalization is made. Also $\\int_\\mathbb T(1-\\psi^2)\\,\\mathrm da<\\varepsilon$.",
          "source_id": "PM-144.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For any strictly positive smooth probability density $\\rho$ on $\\mathbb R$ and any $\\varepsilon>0$, there are a smooth real circle function $J$, an open arc $I'$ with $|\\mathbb T\\setminus I'|<\\varepsilon$ and $J'>0$ on $I'$, and $0\\le\\psi\\le1$ in $C_c^\\infty(I')$, such that $$J_*(\\psi^2\\,\\mathrm da)=\\widetilde\\rho(y)\\,\\mathrm dy,\n \\qquad \\widetilde\\rho\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R),\n \\qquad \\|\\rho-\\widetilde\\rho\\|_1<\\varepsilon.$$ Here the cutoff law is a subprobability measure; no renormalization is made.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-neutral",
        "exact_quote": "Given a twist, its fixed physical chart, a finite disjoint smooth packet list with color densities $p_r$, a positive integer $P$, and a positive tolerance, one can perform a passage with the following simultaneous properties. Its physical map and inverse lie in any prescribed smooth neighborhood of the old ones. Its new good-arc complement is arbitrarily small. Every color vector is transported within the prescribed $L^2$ tolerance, retaining constant tags and disjointness. The new packet moduli are independent of the new spare coordinate. The output densities $p'_r$ and the densities $p'_{r,h}$ contributed by output indices $j\\equiv h\\pmod P$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:neutral-densities}\n \\|p'_r-p_r\\|_1<\\varepsilon,\n \\qquad \\|p'_{r,h}-p_r/P\\|_1<\\varepsilon\n \\quad(0\\le h<P),$$ where $\\varepsilon>0$ may be prescribed arbitrarily. The integer $c$ may first be required to exceed any fixed bound, and the retained supports may use any sufficiently small fixed enlargements of <a href=\"#eq:stationary-image\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:stationary-image\">[eq:stationary-image]</a>. If colors were disjoint in their old angular indices alone, their output angular index sets are still disjoint. The final passage integer $N$ may exceed any prescribed bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-144.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rem-finite-family-passages",
        "exact_quote": "A common passage can handle finitely many packet lists, requiring disjointness only within each list. Choose $c$ using the union of their old indices, choose the neutral cutoff and slope to meet all their finitely many mass and density bounds, and choose $N$ above all the resulting lower bounds. Support cutoffs are chosen separately for each list, disjoint within that list. The norm and density conclusions then hold for every list; no orthogonality between different lists is asserted.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-signal",
        "exact_quote": "For the fixed data $b,D_0,d$ there are an integer $P\\ge2$ and a number $\\gamma_0>0$ with the following property. Any fixed $0<\\gamma\\le\\gamma_0$ can be used, independently of $B$.*\n\n\n*Let $S$ be an intermediate map, and let $$F_r=\\sum_{m\\in\\mathcal M_r} A_{r,m}(u,a)\\mathop{\\mathrm{e}}(m\\theta)$$ be finite smooth packet data in its good arc. Suppose that the finite sets $\\mathcal M_r\\subset\\mathbb Z\\setminus\\{0\\}$ are mutually disjoint, $|A_{r,m}(u,a)|$ is independent of $a$, and $b\\le |m|/B\\le D_0$. Write $p_{r,m}$ for the spectral density of the indicated packet. Given any $\\varepsilon,\\delta_x,\\delta_z>0$ and prescribed positive bounds for the physical $L^2$ transport errors, a passage produces finite output packet sums $F_{r,m}^+$, approximating the corresponding input packets in physical $L^2$, whose spectral densities satisfy $$\\sum_{r,m}\\left\\|p_{r,m}^+\n -p_{r,m}(z)\\left[1+2\\gamma\\mathbf 1_{\\gcd(m,P)=1}\n       \\mathop{\\mathrm{Re}}\\{d(m/B)\\mathop{\\mathrm{e}}(-Nz)\\}\\right]\\right\\|_{L^1(\\mathbb T)}\n <\\varepsilon.\n \\label{eq:signal-conclusion}$$ The new scale is $B^+=NcB$, and the new speed is $\\sigma_N$ from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:passage\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:passage\">2</a>. For every point $(u',a')$ in the coefficient support of a retained output packet of index $j$ descending from $(r,m)$, there is $(u,a)\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}A_{r,m}$ such that $$\\left|\\frac{j}{B^+}-\\frac mB\\right|<\\delta_x,\n \\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_\\mathbb T\\bigl(j\\sigma_N(u'),m\\phi(u)\\bigr)<\\delta_z.\n \\label{eq:signal-support-closeness}$$ Its modulus is independent of the new spare coordinate. Distinct old angular indices have disjoint output angular-index supports. Conjugate symmetry is preserved when present.*\n\n*The passage and its inverse can lie in any prescribed smooth neighborhood of $S$ and $S^{-1}$ in physical coordinates, and the new good arc can have arbitrarily small complement. The choices can be made in the order $c$, a bounded smooth jitter and cutoffs, and finally the integer $N$. After the preceding choices, every sufficiently large integer $N$ works. In particular an arbitrary lower bound on $N$ can be imposed.",
        "source_id": "PM-144.1"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-signal",

For every point $(u',a')$ in the coefficient support of a retained output packet of index $j$ descending from $(r,m)$, there is $(u,a)\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}A_{r,m}$ such that $$\\left|\\frac{j}{B^+}-\\frac mB\\right|<\\delta_x,\n \\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_\\mathbb T\\bigl(j\\sigma_N(u'),m\\phi(u)\\bigr)<\\delta_z.\n \\label{eq:signal-support-closeness}$$ Its modulus is independent of the new spare coordinate.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-refresh-transmit",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $b,D_0,d,P,\\gamma$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:signal\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:signal\">7</a>, and put $$\\lambda=\\gamma\\frac{|(\\mathbb Z/P\\mathbb Z)^\\times|}{P}>0.\n \\label{eq:signal-lambda}$$ Suppose finite colored packet data have mutually disjoint angular-index sets and moduli independent of the spare coordinate. Suppose their normalized indices lie in a fixed compact subset of $\\{x:b<|x|<D_0\\}$. Let $p_r$ be the color densities, and let $d_r:\\mathbb T\\to\\mathbb C$ be continuous functions such that on the support of every input packet of color $r$, $$|d(m/B)-d_r(m\\phi(u)\\bmod1)|\\le\\omega.\n \\label{eq:signal-color-match}$$ Then a neutral passage followed by a transmission gives output colors with $$\\sum_r\\left\\|p_r^+\n  -p_r(z)\\bigl[1+2\\lambda\\mathop{\\mathrm{Re}}\\{d_r(z)\\mathop{\\mathrm{e}}(-Nz)\\}\\bigr]\n                 \\right\\|_1\n \\le 2\\gamma\\omega\\sum_r\\|p_r\\|_1+\\varepsilon,\n \\label{eq:signal-refreshed}$$ where $\\varepsilon>0$ is arbitrary and $N$ is the second passage integer. The physical map and inverse, the physical packet vectors, and the normalized-index and temporal-frequency displacements can all be controlled with arbitrary prescribed positive error bounds. Output packet moduli remain independent of the spare coordinate, and colors remain disjoint in angular indices. The final $N$ can exceed any prescribed bound. Conjugate-symmetric data can be treated conjugate-symmetrically.*\n\n*If different colors start in separated compact normalized-index sets, any fixed sufficiently small enlargements of those sets can contain their outputs. The same conclusion holds through any fixed finite number of such pairs, with positive margins around the band $b<|x|<D_0$. If the input supports satisfy $\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_\\mathbb T(m/B,m\\phi(u))\\le\\kappa$, the output supports satisfy the same inequality with $\\kappa$ increased by an arbitrarily small amount.",
        "source_id": "PM-144.1"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-refresh-transmit",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $b,D_0,d,P,\\gamma$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:signal\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:signal\">7</a>, and put $$\\lambda=\\gamma\\frac{|(\\mathbb Z/P\\mathbb Z)^\\times|}{P}>0.\n \\label{eq:signal-lambda}$$ Suppose finite colored packet data have mutually disjoint angular-index sets and moduli independent of the spare coordinate. Let $p_r$ be the color densities, and let $d_r:\\mathbb T\\to\\mathbb C$ be continuous functions such that on the support of every input packet of color $r$, $$|d(m/B)-d_r(m\\phi(u)\\bmod1)|\\le\\omega.\n \\label{eq:signal-color-match}$$ Then a neutral passage followed by a transmission gives output colors with $$\\sum_r\\left\\|p_r^+\n  -p_r(z)\\bigl[1+2\\lambda\\mathop{\\mathrm{Re}}\\{d_r(z)\\mathop{\\mathrm{e}}(-Nz)\\}\\bigr]\n                 \\right\\|_1\n \\le 2\\gamma\\omega\\sum_r\\|p_r\\|_1+\\varepsilon,\n \\label{eq:signal-refreshed}$$ where $\\varepsilon>0$ is arbitrary and $N$ is the second passage integer.       "exact_statement": "Fix $b,D_0,d,P,\\gamma$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:signal\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:signal\">7</a>, and put $$\\lambda=\\gamma\\frac{|(\\mathbb Z/P\\mathbb Z)^\\times|}{P}>0.\n \\label{eq:signal-lambda}$$ Suppose finite colored packet data have mutually disjoint angular-index sets and moduli independent of the spare coordinate. If the input supports satisfy $\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_\\mathbb T(m/B,m\\phi(u))\\le\\kappa$, the output supports satisfy the same inequality with $\\kappa$ increased by an arbitrarily small amount.",
      "explanation": "Placing a neutral passage before transmission removes the coprimality restriction at the cost of a fixed factor in the signal strength. Colors receive a modulation given by a continuous function with controlled error.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-prediction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be an intermediate map, expressed in a fixed physical volume-preserving coordinate chart. Let $$F_r=\\sum_{m\\in\\mathcal M_r} A_{r,m}(u,a)\\mathop{\\mathrm{e}}(m\\theta),\n \\qquad F=\\sum_r F_r,$$ be a finite list of colors of smooth packets supported compactly in the good arc. Suppose that the finite sets $\\mathcal M_r\\subset\\mathbb Z\\setminus\\{0\\}$ are pairwise disjoint, that $|A_{r,m}(u,a)|$ is independent of $a$, and that the list is invariant under complex conjugation: $F_{r^*}=\\overline{F_r}$. In particular, $F$ is real. Let $p_r$ be the corresponding spectral densities, put $p=\\sum_r p_r$, and suppose that $$\\int_\\mathbb Tp(z)\\,\\,\\mathrm dz\\le \\frac32.$$ Assign constants $b_r\\in\\mathbb C$ satisfying $b_{r^*}=\\overline{b_r}$.*\n\n\n*Given $\\varepsilon,\\tau,\\kappa>0$ and any neighborhood of the physical map and its inverse in the smooth topology, there are a finite sequence of passages, a final intermediate map $S'$, final packets $F'_r$, and a smooth function $\\Psi:\\mathbb T\\to\\mathbb C$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sum_r\\|F'_r-F_r\\|_2&<\\varepsilon,\n \\label{eq:prediction-transport}\\\\\n \\int_\\mathbb T\\sqrt{p'(z)}\\,\\,\\mathrm dz\n &\\ge \\int_\\mathbb T\\sqrt{p(z)}\\,\\,\\mathrm dz-\\tau,\n \\qquad p'=\\sum_r p'_r,\n \\label{eq:prediction-hellinger}\\\\\n \\left\\|\\sum_r b_rF'_r-\n       \\Psi(U_{S'})\\sum_r F'_r\\right\\|_2&<\\varepsilon.\n \\label{eq:prediction-error}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here the differences in <a href=\"#eq:prediction-transport\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:prediction-transport\">[eq:prediction-transport]</a> are measured in the original physical $L^2$ space. All the physical maps and their inverses can be kept in the prescribed neighborhood. The final good arc $I'$ satisfies $|\\mathbb T\\setminus I'|<\\kappa$. The output colors again have disjoint nonzero angular-index sets, moduli independent of the spare coordinate, and conjugation symmetry. The multiplier can be chosen with $\\Psi(-z)=\\overline{\\Psi(z)}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-144.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-prediction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be an intermediate map, expressed in a fixed physical volume-preserving coordinate chart. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Koopman operator convention** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For an invertible measure-preserving transformation $T$, we use the convention $U_Tg=g\circ T$.
- **Mean-zero space** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The closed subspace of complex square-integrable functions of integral zero is denoted by $L^2_0(\mathbb T^3,\mu)$.
- **Packets and colors** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We refer to its individual harmonics as *packets*, and keep a finite label, or *color*, on disjoint groups of packets.
- **Jitter** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The spare coordinate $a$ supplies a small adjustable variation in the speed (the *jitter*).
- **Volume-preserving chart** ([source](#eq:twist)): A volume-preserving coordinate system, also called a chart below, means a smooth volume-preserving identification of the physical torus with $\mathbb T^3$.
- **Good arc** ([source](#eq:twist)): We call $I$ a good arc.
- **Packet** ([source](#sec:passages)): A packet is a function $A(u,a)\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(m\theta)$, where $m\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}$ and $A$ is smooth with compact support in $I\times\mathbb T$.
- **Disjoint packet list** ([source](#sec:passages)): A finite packet list is disjoint if, for any two entries with the same $m$, their coefficient supports are disjoint.
- **Spectral density convention** ([source](#eq:packet-spectral-measure)): We use the inner product linear in its first variable, so a vector of density $p$ has $\langle U_S^nF,F\rangle=\int_\mathbb T\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(nz)p(z)\,\mathrm dz$.
- **Total variation norm of densities** ([source](#eq:density-norm-bound)): The total variation norm here and below is the $L^1$ norm of densities, without a factor $1/2$.
- **Scale and normalized index** ([source](#eq:signal-d)): A scale is a positive real number $B$, and the normalized index of a packet at angular index $m$ is $x=m/B$.
- **Laurent polynomial** ([source](#sec:blocks)): In the following lemma, a Laurent polynomial in $w_1,\ldots,w_n$ means a finite linear combination of the monomials $w^\nu=\prod_i w_i^{\nu_i}$, with $\nu\in\mathbb Z^n$.

      "exact_definition": "A scale is a positive real number $B$, and the normalized index of a packet at angular index $m$ is $x=m/B$.",
      "interpretation": "Signal strength depends only on the normalized index, not on how large the angular indices have become.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Scale and normalized index",
      "scope": "Section 3 and block construction."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:blocks",

          "anchor": "sec:blocks",

          "source_id": "PM-144.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "In the following lemma, a Laurent polynomial in $w_1,\\ldots,w_n$ means a finite linear combination of the monomials $w^\\nu=\\prod_i w_i^{\\nu_i}$, with $\\nu\\in\\mathbb Z^n$.",
      "interpretation": "Finite sums of monomials with integer, possibly negative, exponents.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Laurent polynomial",
      "scope": "Phase-test lemma and its use in blocks."
    }

}

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-passage** [claim](#stmt-lem-passage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-passage): Explicit coordinate passage, stationary phase for the angular coefficients, then fine-scale density computation. -> new twist with transported packets and fine temporal density
- **step-fast-average** [claim](#stmt-lem-fast-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fast-average): Partition into periods and compare with Riemann sums using uniform continuity. -> averaged limit and diagonal comparison of periodized laws
- **step-law-approx** [claim](#stmt-lem-law-approx) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-law-approx): Invert the distribution function and cut off near the endpoints. -> jitter function and cutoff realizing a nearby law
- **step-neutral** [claim](#stmt-lem-neutral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-neutral): Take a steep linear jitter so that the periodized density is a fine Riemann sum, then apply the passage. -> residue-balanced output densities
- **step-finite-family** [claim](#stmt-rem-finite-family-passages) / [proof](#stmt-rem-finite-family-passages): Choose the shared index shift from the union of old indices and use separate cutoffs per list. -> common passage valid for every list
- **step-signal** [claim](#stmt-lem-signal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-signal): Build a positive law by a Fourier-supported kernel, use a prime sieve for the residue characters, and realize the law by a passage. -> density modulated by a small sinusoid
- **step-refresh-transmit** [claim](#stmt-lem-refresh-transmit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-refresh-transmit): Neutral refresh to equalize residue classes, then transmission, with the tag substituted by a continuous function. -> color densities modulated with the effective strength
- **step-phase-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-phase-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-phase-tests): Riemann–Lebesgue decay of Fourier coefficients and rapidly growing frequencies. -> diagonal averages close to product averages
- **step-prediction** [claim](#stmt-prop-prediction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-phase-tests-2): Blocks of refresh–transmit pairs centered at a smooth mean, second-moment control, phase tests, and averaging of block estimators. -> multiplier predicting the tags with small square-root loss
- **step-flat-vector** [claim](#stmt-lem-flat-vector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-flat-vector): Explicit two-mode vector with amplitude matched to the speed derivative. -> real centered vector of density one
- **step-stage** [claim](#stmt-prop-stage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-stage): Prepare packets, tag them by constant ratios on rectangles, separate colors, predict, then whiten spectrally. -> new admissible pair and Laurent polynomial
- **step-main-limit** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-stage-2): Diagonal induction over a dense family of observables with summable errors and a smooth limit. -> limit diffeomorphism and orthonormal complete orbit
- **step-consequences** [claim](#stmt-cor-dynamical-profile) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-dynamical-profile): Riemann–Lebesgue for mixing, cited multiple-mixing, finite-multiplicity entropy and Pesin formula theorems. -> mixing, zero entropy and vanishing exponents

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.roadmap.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-simple-lebesgue-orbit**: A smooth volume-preserving diffeomorphism of the three-torus exists together with a real generator whose bilateral Koopman orbit is an orthonormal basis of the whole mean-zero square-integrable space; hence the map is ergodic and the Koopman operator is unitarily the multiplication model on the circle.
- **inv-zero-entropy-zero-exponents**: For the constructed map, the Kolmogorov-Sinai entropy vanishes and all three Lyapunov exponents vanish almost everywhere, while mixing of every finite order holds.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-chaotic-model**: Use the map as a model of chaotic dynamics with positive entropy or exponential sensitivity. -> The map mixes in every order yet has zero entropy and vanishing Lyapunov exponents, so it carries no exponential divergence of orbits.
- **anti-uniform-control**: Read off explicit quantitative control (derivative bounds, packet counts, convergence rates) of the limit map from the construction. -> The construction is an inductive limit in which each stage is chosen after the previous data; no uniform control over successive charts or packet counts is asserted, so no explicit bound for the limit is available.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is an existence statement obtained as a limit of an infinite sequence of stages, each chosen after the previous data, with no uniform bound on packets or chart derivatives; no finite procedure outputs the diffeomorphism or its generator, so there is no finite constructive object to execute and certify.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main theorem is an existence result obtained as a limit of infinitely many coordinate passages, each with an integer chosen last after prior data are fixed. No finite program computes the diffeomorphism, so there is nothing to implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns exact spectral properties of a limit map on the torus with infinite-precision arithmetic. Any floating-point simulation of orbits would not retain the Lebesgue spectrum. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper supplies no procedure with explicit input and output that a tool could wrap. Each passage acts on finite data, but the full construction is an infinite inductive limit. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Individual passages and blocks are finite constructions, but the theorem itself is a limit statement whose parameters rely on analytic asymptotics (stationary phase, Fourier tail decay) with no stated complexity. No algorithm with a bound is given. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure is introduced. Packets and colors are analytic bookkeeping devices for Fourier modes, not storage structures. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns parallel or hardware-level computation. The construction is purely analytic with no computational model. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The corollary gives zero entropy and vanishing Lyapunov exponents, so the map has no diffusion or unpredictability in the cryptographic sense, despite mixing of all orders. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no learning problem, data or loss. The prediction and whitening steps are operator-theoretic approximations inside L^2, not statistical estimators trained on samples. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no objective to optimise. Parameter choices (block count, strength, budget) merely balance error terms in a proof. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communication, replication or concurrency appears anywhere in the construction. |
| other | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | As a mathematical reference, the result supplies a smooth volume-preserving model with simple Lebesgue spectrum and mixing of all orders but zero entropy. It can serve as a benchmark or counterexample in spectral and ergodic theory. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-existence-only** (nonconstructive): The main theorem is an existence result obtained as a limit of an infinite inductive sequence of stages, each chosen after the earlier data are fixed. It gives no finite procedure that outputs the diffeomorphism or the generator. Blocked use: ['Extracting executable code or explicit parameters for the limit map', 'Quoting running times or convergence rates for the construction']
- **limit-no-uniform-control** (hypothesis): No uniform bound on the number of packets or on the derivatives of successive coordinate charts is assumed, so no quantitative control of the limit map follows from the construction. Blocked use: ['Reading off derivative bounds or packet counts of the limit map', 'Claiming uniform convergence rates for the successive approximations']
- **limit-passage-order-of-choices** (hypothesis): Each passage integer is chosen last, after the current chart, cutoffs, jitter and all error bounds are fixed, so a passage is valid only for the finite data it was built for. Blocked use: ['Reusing one passage integer across different packet lists or charts without re-choosing it', 'Treating a passage as uniform in the scale or in the packet list']
- **limit-signal-constant-index** (hypothesis): The signal lemma requires normalized indices in a fixed band, packet moduli independent of the spare coordinate, and mutually disjoint index sets for the colors. Blocked use: ['Applying transmission to packets whose normalized indices leave the band', 'Applying it when packet moduli depend on the spare coordinate']
- **limit-prediction-mass-ceiling** (hypothesis): The prediction proposition assumes finitely many colors of smooth packets with disjoint index sets, conjugation symmetry and total density mass at most one and a half. Blocked use: ['Applying prediction to data whose total density mass exceeds the ceiling', 'Applying it to non-conjugation-symmetric color lists']
- **limit-zero-entropy** (edge_case): The constructed map has zero entropy and all Lyapunov exponents vanish almost everywhere, despite the spectral model, so it has no exponential instability. Blocked use: ['Using the map as a model of chaos with positive entropy', 'Using it where cryptographic diffusion is required']
- **limit-multiple-mixing-external** (formal_scope): Mixing of every order beyond ordinary mixing rests on a separate cited multiple-mixing theorem, not on the spectral argument itself. Blocked use: ['Attributing all-order mixing to the spectral construction alone']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 144 (`lean/docs/144.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A smooth three-torus diffeomorphism with simple Lebesgue spectrum](../../preprints/A-smooth-three-torus-diffeomorphism-with-simple-Lebesgue-spectrum-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Banach's simple Lebesgue-spectrum problem asks for a probability-preserving transformation with simple Lebesgue spectrum on its mean-zero $L^2$ space. The formalization constructs a smooth volume-preserving diffeomorphism of the three-torus with this property. It gives a real-valued cyclic vector whose integer iterates form an orthonormal basis of the entire mean-zero space, and an isometric identification under which the Koopman operator becomes multiplication by the coordinate function on the circle. The transformation is also ergodic.

Comparator source `ThreeTorus.lean` (2995 bytes, sha256 `f73e804548a3348440244dd4f3e382acefeb47c48146f374d6dea5cdfdc83f12`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-!
The torus is (ℝ/ℤ)³ with normalized product Haar measure. Smoothness means
local smooth lifts on the universal cover, for both the map and its inverse.

The almost-everywhere graph conditions are independent of representatives of
L² classes. The real generator's bilateral Koopman orbit spans the entire
complex mean-zero L² space. The spectral model is the full circle L² space
with multiplication by the first Fourier character.
-/

noncomputable section
open MeasureTheory Set
open scoped ENNReal Topology

namespace ThreeTorus

abbrev Torus := Fin 3 → UnitAddCircle
abbrev Cover := Fin 3 → ℝ

def project (x : Cover) : Torus := fun i => (x i : UnitAddCircle)

def Smooth (T : Torus → Torus) : Prop :=
  ∀ x : Cover, ∃ U : Set Cover, IsOpen U ∧ x ∈ U ∧
    ∃ F : Cover → Cover, ContDiffOn ℝ ((⊤ : ℕ∞) : WithTop ℕ∞) F U ∧
      ∀ y ∈ U, T (project y) = project (F y)

def μ : Measure Torus := Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin 3 => AddCircle.haarAddCircle)

instance : IsProbabilityMeasure μ := by unfold μ; infer_instance

abbrev H : Type := Lp ℂ 2 μ

/-- The full complex subspace defined by zero Lebesgue integral. -/
def meanZero : Submodule ℂ H where
  carrier := {f | ∫ x, f x ∂μ = 0}
  zero_mem' := by
    change ∫ x, (0 : H) x ∂μ = 0
    rw [integral_congr_ae (Lp.coeFn_zero ℂ 2 μ)]
    simp
  add_mem' := by
    intro f g hf hg
    change ∫ x, (f + g) x ∂μ = 0
    rw [integral_congr_ae (Lp.coeFn_add f g)]
    change ∫ x, f x + g x ∂μ = 0
    rw [integral_add (MemLp.integrable (by norm_num) (Lp.memLp f))
      (MemLp.integrable (by norm_num) (Lp.memLp g))]
    change (∫ x, f x ∂μ) + (∫ x, g x ∂μ) = 0
    rw [show ∫ x, f x ∂μ = 0 from hf, show ∫ x, g x ∂μ = 0 from hg, add_zero]
  smul_mem' := by
    intro c f hf
    change ∫ x, (c • f) x ∂μ = 0
    rw [integral_congr_ae (Lp.coeFn_smul c f)]
    change ∫ x, c • f x ∂μ = 0
    rw [integral_smul]
    rw [show ∫ x, f x ∂μ = 0 from hf, smul_zero]

abbrev H₀ := ↥meanZero
abbrev CircleH : Type := Lp ℂ 2 (AddCircle.haarAddCircle (T := (1 : ℝ)))

def IsReal (f : H₀) : Prop := ∀ᵐ x ∂μ, (f.1 x).im = 0

/-- Complete simple Lebesgue spectrum, including the stated consequences. -/
def MainConclusion : Prop :=
  ∃ T : Equiv.Perm Torus,
    Smooth T ∧ Smooth T.symm ∧ MeasurePreserving T μ μ ∧
    ∃ f : H₀, IsReal f ∧
    ∃ v : ℤ → H₀,
      (∀ n : ℤ, ∀ᵐ x ∂μ, (v n).1 x = f.1 ((T ^ n) x)) ∧
      Orthonormal ℂ v ∧
      Dense (Submodule.span ℂ (Set.range v) : Set H₀) ∧
      Ergodic T μ ∧
      ∃ W : H₀ ≃ₗᵢ[ℂ] CircleH,
        ∀ g : H₀, ∃ h : H₀,
          (∀ᵐ x ∂μ, h.1 x = g.1 (T x)) ∧
          (∀ᵐ z ∂(AddCircle.haarAddCircle (T := (1 : ℝ))),
            W h z = fourier 1 z * W g z)

theorem simple_lebesgue_spectrum : MainConclusion := by
  sorry

end ThreeTorus

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We solve the probability-preserving form of Banach's simple Lebesgue-spectrum problem in smooth dynamics. We construct a <i>C</i><sup>∞</sup> diffeomorphism of the three-torus that preserves standard volume and whose Koopman operator has simple Lebesgue spectrum on the entire mean-zero <i>L</i><sup>2</sup> space.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $\mathbb T=\mathbb R/\mathbb Z$, let $\mu$ be normalized Lebesgue measure on $\mathbb T^3$, and put $\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(t)=\exp(2\pi i t)$. For an invertible measure-preserving transformation $T$, we use the convention $U_Tg=g\circ T$. The closed subspace of complex square-integrable functions of integral zero is denoted by $L^2_0(\mathbb T^3,\mu)$.

On a probability space the constants form an invariant subspace, so the probability-preserving form of Banach’s simple Lebesgue-spectrum problem asks for an invertible transformation whose Koopman operator on the *entire* orthogonal complement of the constants is unitarily equivalent to multiplication by $w$ on $L^2(S^1,m)$, where $m$ is normalized Haar measure. Equivalently, that restriction has a bilateral orthonormal orbit which is complete. We realize this spectral model with the standard smooth volume on a compact manifold.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There exist a $C^\infty$ volume-preserving diffeomorphism $T\colon\mathbb T^3\to\mathbb T^3$ and a real function $f\in L^2_0(\mathbb T^3,\mu)$ such that $$\{f\circ T^n:n\in\mathbb Z\}$$ is an orthonormal basis of $L^2_0(\mathbb T^3,\mu)$. Consequently $T$ is ergodic, and $U_T$ on this space is unitarily equivalent to multiplication by the coordinate $w$ on $L^2(S^1,m)$.*

</div>

The invariant measure in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> is the standard smooth volume itself. The generator $f$ is only required to belong to $L^2$. In particular, the assertion concerns the whole mean-zero space, including both its spectral type and its multiplicity. Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">6</a> derives mixing, zero entropy, and vanishing Lyapunov exponents. Mixing of every order uses, in addition, the separate multiple-mixing theorem of (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[MultipleMixing2026](#ref-MultipleMixing2026)].

The formulation above is distinct from the historical real-line question recorded by Ulam and attributed to Banach (Ulam 1960, vol. 8, sec. 6, p. 76) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Ulam1960](#ref-Ulam1960)]. Rokhlin asked for ergodic automorphisms with simple, or at least finite-multiplicity, Lebesgue spectrum (Rokhlin 1949, sec. 4, no. 7, p. 107) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Rokhlin1949](#ref-Rokhlin1949)]. Helson and Parry constructed cocycles over aperiodic probability-preserving transformations whose associated unitary operators have Lebesgue spectrum (Helson and Parry 1978, 196, Theorem (a)) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[HelsonParry1978](#ref-HelsonParry1978)]. Mathew and Nadkarni constructed a probability-preserving transformation with a Lebesgue *component* of multiplicity two (Mathew and Nadkarni 1984) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[MathewNadkarni1984](#ref-MathewNadkarni1984)]. Guenais connected the Morse-cocycle question with flat polynomials and constructed a countable abelian group action with simple spectrum of mixed discrete and Haar type (Guenais 1999) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Guenais1999](#ref-Guenais1999)]. These results distinguish a Lebesgue component, or a related group action, from one bilateral Lebesgue orbit spanning the whole centered space.

Simple spectrum in smooth dynamics was already achieved by Fayad, who constructed $C^\infty$ volume-preserving torus diffeomorphisms with simple spectrum (B. R. Fayad 2001) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Fayad2001](#ref-Fayad2001)]; that result did not assert Lebesgue spectral type. In another setting, an infinite-measure conservative transformation with simple Lebesgue spectrum is presented in (Abdalaoui 2023, Theorem 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[ElAbdalaoui2023](#ref-ElAbdalaoui2023)]; the probability-space conclusion does not follow from it. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> gives that conclusion for the full mean-zero space of a smooth probability-preserving map.

Related existence results in continuous time have different spectral consequences. Prikhod’ko constructs a finite-measure flow with simple Lebesgue spectrum (Prikhod’ko 2020, Theorem 3) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Prikhodko2020](#ref-Prikhodko2020)]. In the smooth category, Fayad, Forni and Kanigowski obtain a real analytic conservative flow on $\mathbb T^2$ with one singularity and countably infinite Lebesgue multiplicity (B. Fayad, Forni, and Kanigowski 2021, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[FFK2021](#ref-FFK2021)]; Abdedou, Fayad and Kessi obtain real analytic reparametrizations of minimal translation flows on $\mathbb T^5$ with the same multiplicity (Abdedou, Fayad, and Kessi 2023, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[AFK2023](#ref-AFK2023)]. Taking a nonzero time map of a simple Lebesgue flow yields countably infinite multiplicity: its spectral model is multiplication by $\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(t\xi)$ on $L^2(\mathbb R,\,\mathrm d\xi)$, and the substitution $\xi=(x+k)/|t|$, with $x\in[0,1)$ and $k\in\mathbb Z$, gives one Lebesgue copy for each $k$. Thus these continuous-time results do not supply Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

Successive smooth changes of coordinates have a substantial history in smooth dynamics, including the approximation-by-conjugacy constructions of Anosov and Katok (Anosov and Katok 1970) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[AnosovKatok1970](#ref-AnosovKatok1970)]. We use explicit volume-preserving passages between integrable twists rather than an external conjugacy theorem. Their quantitative spectral-density transport, and the finite Fourier signals built on it, are proved below.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The construction

The approximating maps are integrable twists. In a volume-preserving coordinate system on the torus, they have the form $$S(\theta,u,a)=(\theta+\phi(u),u,a),$$ where $\phi$ is a smooth real circle function that is strictly increasing on an arc whose complement can be made arbitrarily short. A finite angular Fourier expansion supported on that arc has an explicit absolutely continuous spectral measure. We refer to its individual harmonics as *packets*, and keep a finite label, or *color*, on disjoint groups of packets. The colors carry the coefficients of an observable that we want to approximate by translates of a single vector.

There are three steps. A slow passage in the coordinate $u$ replaces the twist by an arbitrarily close twist whose angular coordinate is the old $u$. Stationary phase locates the new angular indices and determines the spectral density at a much finer scale. The spare coordinate $a$ supplies a small adjustable variation in the speed (the *jitter*). A large integer added to the lift of $\phi$ separates the angular indices of different packets without changing the old map. This separation is what allows later perturbations to distinguish colors even when their temporal spectra overlap.

Next, a positive Fourier construction and a finite prime sieve produce a small sinusoidal bias in each color density. Its coefficient depends on the normalized angular index. A neutral passage between successive biased passages makes residue classes carry equal shares of each color. The bias strength can be chosen before the angular scale grows; the passage parameter is then free to enforce any required smooth or spectral accuracy.

Finally, many small biases are grouped into blocks. If color $r$ has spectral density $p_r$ and target coefficient $b_r$, the center at a temporal frequency $z$ is the density-weighted mean $$p(z)=\sum_r p_r(z),\qquad
 \beta(z)=\frac{\sum_r b_rp_r(z)}{p(z)}\quad(p(z)>0).$$ Centering each block at a smooth approximation to this current mean makes the loss in $\int\sqrt p$ quadratic in the block’s strength budget, while the same block provides an estimator of the color label. Repetition makes prediction arbitrarily accurate with arbitrarily little loss in $\int\sqrt p$. This separation of the two estimates is the central finite construction. When the vector’s norm is close to one, keeping $\int\sqrt p$ close to one makes whitening a small change in physical $L^2$, while restoring the spectral density exactly to one. A diagonal limit preserves the orthonormal orbit and makes its span contain every member of a dense family of observables.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Order of the proof

Section <a href="#sec:passages" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:passages">2</a> proves the analytic passage rules, including the comparison of jitter laws along a rapidly oscillating phase. Section <a href="#sec:signals" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:signals">3</a> constructs the positive laws that transmit the small biases. Section <a href="#sec:blocks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:blocks">4</a> proves the finite prediction result, and Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">5</a> prepares arbitrary observables and takes the smooth limit. Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">6</a> derives the dynamical consequences. Every passage acts on finite data. Its integer parameter is chosen last, after the current coordinate chart, cutoffs, jitter, and all desired error bounds have been fixed. No uniform bound on the number of packets or on the derivatives of successive coordinate charts is assumed.

<a id="sec:passages"></a>
### Smooth passages and spectral densities

<a id="eq:twist"></a>
A volume-preserving coordinate system, also called a chart below, means a smooth volume-preserving identification of the physical torus with $\mathbb T^3$. All coordinate systems in the construction preserve normalized volume. An intermediate transformation has the form $$\label{eq:twist}
 S(\theta,u,a)=(\theta+\phi(u),u,a),
 \qquad (\theta,u,a)\in\mathbb T^3,$$ where $\phi\in C^\infty(\mathbb T;\mathbb R)$ has $\phi'>0$ on an open arc $I$. We call $I$ a good arc. Derivatives on an arc are understood in an increasing real lift of that arc.

A packet is a function $A(u,a)\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(m\theta)$, where $m\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}$ and $A$ is smooth with compact support in $I\times\mathbb T$. A finite packet list is disjoint if, for any two entries with the same $m$, their coefficient supports are disjoint. Entries with different $m$ need no support restriction. Packets may be grouped into colors; we write $F_r$ for the sum of color $r$. Disjointness implies <a id="eq:packet-orthogonality"></a>$$\label{eq:packet-orthogonality}
 \langle U_S^n F_r,U_S^k F_l\rangle=0
 \quad(r\ne l,\ n,k\in\mathbb Z).$$ Indeed $U_S^n(A\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(m\theta))=\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(nm\phi(u))A\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(m\theta)$, and either angular orthogonality or disjoint coefficient supports applies. The spectral measure of one packet is <a id="eq:packet-spectral-measure"></a>$$\label{eq:packet-spectral-measure}
 (m\phi\bmod1)_*\bigl(|A(u,a)|^2\,\mathrm du\,\mathrm da\bigr).$$ It has a smooth density because its coefficient support is compactly inside the good arc. We use the inner product linear in its first variable, so a vector of density $p$ has $\langle U_S^nF,F\rangle=\int_\mathbb T\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(nz)p(z)\,\mathrm dz$. A measurable spectral multiplier $v(z)$ acts on its coefficient by multiplication by $v(m\phi(u)\bmod1)$, whenever the result belongs to $L^2$. These statements follow directly from the displayed formula for $U_S^n$, first for trigonometric polynomials and then by $L^2$ approximation. The pushforward formula, absolute continuity, and multiplier rule also apply to nonsmooth coefficients in $L^2$ supported in the good arc; their densities need not be smooth.

<a id="eq:density-norm-bound"></a>
For later comparisons, if two vectors have spectral densities $p_F,p_G$ for the same transformation, then $$\label{eq:density-norm-bound}
 \|p_F-p_G\|_1
 \le (\|F\|_2+\|G\|_2)\|F-G\|_2.$$ To see this, test their spectral measures against an arbitrary function of absolute value at most one and use the contraction property of its spectral multiplier. The total variation norm here and below is the $L^1$ norm of densities, without a factor $1/2$.

<a id="stmt-lem-passage"></a>
<div id="lem:passage" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:passage-rounding"></a>
<a id="eq:passage-map"></a>
**Lemma 2** (Passage). *Fix <a href="#eq:twist" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:twist">[eq:twist]</a>, its physical volume-preserving coordinate chart, and a finite disjoint packet list. Choose a positive integer $c$ so large that the compact ranges $m(c+\phi(\mathbb T))$, for the distinct old indices $m$, are pairwise disjoint and avoid zero. Set $V=c+\phi$. Fix $J\in C^\infty(\mathbb T;\mathbb R)$ with $J'>0$ on an open arc $I'$ and fix $0\le\psi\le1$ in $C_c^\infty(I')$. For integers $N\to\infty$, choose $\Delta_N$ so that $$\label{eq:passage-rounding}
 |\Delta_N|\le N^{-1},\qquad
 k_N:=N\left(\int_\mathbb TV(u)\,\mathrm du+\Delta_N\right)\in\mathbb Z,
 \qquad H_N'=N(V+\Delta_N).$$ Put $\sigma_N(a)=N^{-1}+N^{-2}J(a)$. Then $$\label{eq:passage-map}
 T_N(\theta,u,a)=
 \bigl(\theta+H_N(u+\sigma_N(a))-H_N(u),\,
 u+\sigma_N(a),\,a\bigr)$$ is a volume-preserving smooth torus diffeomorphism, and both $T_N$ and $T_N^{-1}$ converge smoothly to $S$ and $S^{-1}$, also in the fixed physical coordinates. In the new coordinates <a id="eq:passage-chart"></a>$$\label{eq:passage-chart}
 (\theta',u',a')=C_N(\theta,u,a)
             =(u,a,\theta-H_N(u)),$$ it is the twist of speed $\sigma_N$ and good arc $I'$.*

<a id="eq:stationary-image"></a>
<a id="eq:retained-packets"></a>
<a id="eq:exact-passage-coefficient"></a>
<a id="eq:transport-norm"></a>
*Here are its packet and density properties. For an old packet indexed by $\alpha$, with index $m_\alpha$ and amplitude $A_\alpha$, define $$\label{eq:stationary-image}
 E_\alpha=\{(m_\alpha V(u),a):(u,a)\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}A_\alpha\}.$$ One may choose pairwise disjoint, arbitrarily small fixed neighborhoods of these compact sets, and smooth cutoffs $\chi_\alpha(s,a)$ supported in those neighborhoods and equal to one near $E_\alpha$. Retain the finitely many new packets $$\begin{aligned}
 G_{\alpha,j}(\theta',u',a')
   &=\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(j\theta')\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(m_\alpha a')
       \psi(u')\chi_\alpha(j/N,u')C_{\alpha,j}(u'),
       \label{eq:retained-packets}\\
 C_{\alpha,j}(a)
   &=\int_\mathbb TA_\alpha(u,a)\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(m_\alpha H_N(u)-ju)\,\mathrm du.
       \label{eq:exact-passage-coefficient}
\end{aligned}$$ All their angular indices are nonzero for sufficiently large $N$. For each $\alpha$, $$\label{eq:transport-norm}
 \left\|\sum_jG_{\alpha,j}\circ C_N
       -\psi(a)A_\alpha(u,a)\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(m_\alpha\theta)\right\|_2=o(1).$$ They are disjoint as packets, and their moduli are independent of the new spare coordinate $a'$. Every constant attached to an old packet is preserved by this linear transport and these cutoffs.*

<a id="eq:W-definition"></a>
<a id="eq:fine-density-unfolded"></a>
*For a single old packet write, with zero extension outside its branch, $$\label{eq:W-definition}
 W(s,a)=\frac{|A(u,a)|^2}{|m\phi'(u)|},\qquad s=mV(u).$$ Let $K(s,y)$ be the density of the pushforward of $W(s,a)\psi(a)^2\,\mathrm da$ under $a\mapsto sJ(a)$. It is continuous and compactly supported in $(s,y)$, with its $s$ support away from zero. For any fixed positive integer $P$ and residue $h\pmod P$, the unfolded temporal density of the retained packets with $j\equiv h\pmod P$ differs in $L^1(\mathbb R)$ by $o(1)$ from $$\label{eq:fine-density-unfolded}
 Q_{N,h}(t)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z}K(t,Nt-h-kP).$$ Consequently their density on $\mathbb T$ differs in $L^1(\mathbb T)$ by $o(1)$ from $\sum_{l\in\mathbb Z}Q_{N,h}(z+l)$. The assertions hold after summing over any of the finitely many colors. All $o(1)$ terms refer to the old data, $c,J,\psi,P$, and support neighborhoods fixed before $N$ tends to infinity. In particular, $N$ may be required to exceed any prescribed bound.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-passage"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-passage</summary>

*Proof.* *Geometry and smooth convergence.* Choose $k_N$ by rounding $N\int V$ to an integer and integrate <a href="#eq:passage-rounding" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:passage-rounding">[eq:passage-rounding]</a>. Then $H_N(u+1)-H_N(u)=k_N$, so the map $u\mapsto H_N(u)\bmod1$ is globally defined. The map $C_N$ is a smooth torus diffeomorphism of Jacobian one, with inverse $(\theta',u',a')\mapsto(a'+H_N(\theta'),\theta',u')$. Direct substitution gives $$C_NT_NC_N^{-1}(\theta',u',a')
       =(\theta'+\sigma_N(u'),u',a').$$ This proves the global assertions, except convergence. The integral identity $$H_N(u+\sigma_N(a))-H_N(u)
       =N\int_0^{\sigma_N(a)}(V(u+t)+\Delta_N)\,\mathrm dt
       =V(u)+O_{C^k}(N^{-1})$$ holds for each fixed $k$, with constants depending on the fixed $c,J$ and $\phi$. Since $c$ is an integer, addition of $V$ and of $\phi$ agree on $\mathbb T$. The inverse of <a href="#eq:passage-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:passage-map">[eq:passage-map]</a> is $$(\theta,u,a)\longmapsto
 (\theta-H_N(u)+H_N(u-\sigma_N(a)),u-\sigma_N(a),a),$$ and the same estimate proves inverse convergence. Composing with a fixed physical chart and its inverse preserves convergence in every fixed smooth norm. No bound uniform over future coordinate charts is being asserted.

<a id="eq:stationary-uniform"></a>
*Fourier coefficients.* Work on a compact subarc of $I$ containing all old coefficient supports in its interior. For $s=j/N$ the phase in <a href="#eq:exact-passage-coefficient" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exact-passage-coefficient">[eq:exact-passage-coefficient]</a>, divided by $N$, has critical equation <a id="eq:critical-equation"></a>$$\label{eq:critical-equation}
 m(V(u)+\Delta_N)=s$$ and second derivative $m\phi'(u)$. It has at most one critical point on this arc. With $u_*=u_*(s-m\Delta_N)$ on that branch, set $$L_{m,j}(a)=
 \frac{A(u_*,a)}{\sqrt{N|m\phi'(u_*)|}}
 \mathop{\mathrm{e}}\left(mH_N(u_*)-ju_*+\frac{\operatorname{sgn}(m)}8\right),$$ and set this to zero wherever the amplitude at the critical point is zero or there is no point on the branch. Uniformly for $j/N$ in any fixed compact interval and $a\in\mathbb T$, $$\label{eq:stationary-uniform}
 C_{m,j}(a)-L_{m,j}(a)=o(N^{-1/2}).$$

For general stationary-phase background see (Hörmander 2003) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Hormander2003](#ref-Hormander2003)]; we give the compact-support one-dimensional argument needed here. Enlarge the support arc twice inside $I$. Critical points near the original support then range over a fixed compact interval on which the absolute second derivative is bounded below. A smooth change of variable, depending smoothly on the critical point, reduces the divided phase to its critical value plus $\operatorname{sgn}(m)y^2/2$; its Jacobian at zero is $|m\phi'(u_*)|^{-1/2}$. After putting $v=\sqrt N y$, the integral on $|v|\le D$ converges uniformly to the constant amplitude times the quadratic exponential integral there. The remaining local tails are $O(N^{-1/2}/D)$, uniformly in the parameters, by integrating against $\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(\operatorname{sgn}(m)v^2/2)$: the rescaled amplitudes have uniformly bounded suprema and total variation. The part away from the critical point is $O(N^{-1})$ by integration by parts. Finally $$\lim_{D\to\infty}\int_{-D}^D\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(\pm v^2/2)\,\mathrm dv
          =\exp(\pm i\pi/4);$$ this follows by inserting a Gaussian factor, evaluating the Gaussian integral, and removing the factor using the same tail bound. Taking first $N\to\infty$, then $D\to\infty$, proves <a href="#eq:stationary-uniform" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stationary-uniform">[eq:stationary-uniform]</a>. When the potential critical point is outside the enlarged support interval, the integral is uniformly nonstationary. This also covers zero amplitudes and the ends of the branch.

<a id="eq:summed-stationary-mass"></a>
There are $O(N)$ indices in a fixed bounded $j/N$ range. Outside a sufficiently large such range, repeated integration by parts gives, for any fixed positive integer $q$, $|C_{m,j}(a)|\le C_q|j|^{-q}$ uniformly in $a$ and $N$. Indeed the phase derivative has size comparable to $|j|$, and its higher derivatives are $O(N)$ with $N=O(|j|)$ there. Thus <a href="#eq:stationary-uniform" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stationary-uniform">[eq:stationary-uniform]</a> implies $$\sum_j\int_\mathbb T|C_{m,j}-L_{m,j}|^2\,\mathrm da=o(1).$$ The $L^2$ norm of the leading array is bounded by a Riemann sum. Cauchy–Schwarz therefore gives the useful mass estimate $$\label{eq:summed-stationary-mass}
 \sum_j\int_\mathbb T
 \left||C_{m,j}(a)|^2
       -\frac1N W(j/N-m\Delta_N,a)\right|\,\mathrm da=o(1).$$

*Cutoffs and transport.* The map $(u,a)\mapsto(mV(u),a)$ is injective on $I\times\mathbb T$. It takes disjoint compact supports with the same $m$ to disjoint compact sets. Our choice of $c$ separates the images for different $m$. The stated cutoffs $\chi_\alpha$ therefore exist. For large $N$ they equal one on the support of the corresponding leading array, since its $s$ coordinate is shifted by only $m_\alpha\Delta_N$. Because each cutoff is one on the shifted leading-array support, the deleted exact coefficient mass is bounded by the summed squared $C_{m,j}-L_{m,j}$ error, which tends to zero by the preceding estimate and Parseval’s identity. The same argument is valid after multiplication by $\psi$, proving <a href="#eq:transport-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-norm">[eq:transport-norm]</a> and <a href="#eq:summed-stationary-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:summed-stationary-mass">[eq:summed-stationary-mass]</a> for the retained arrays with the factor $\psi^2$ on the right. The cutoffs make separation exact, not merely asymptotic. Different old $m$ never contribute to the same retained $j$, and at a shared $j$ the remaining supports in $u'=a$ are disjoint. The factor $\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(ma')$ in <a href="#eq:retained-packets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:retained-packets">[eq:retained-packets]</a> proves the asserted independence of the new spare-coordinate modulus, also after combining packets at one index. Linearity proves the statement about attached constants.

<a id="eq:rounding-mass-removal"></a>
*Fine temporal density.* We first remove rounding before any fine-scale sampling. Smoothness and compact support of the fixed $W$ give $$\label{eq:rounding-mass-removal}
 \frac1N\sum_j\int_\mathbb T
 |W(j/N-m\Delta_N,a)-W(j/N,a)|\,\mathrm da=o(1).$$ For example, a uniform bound on $\partial_s W$ and $O(N)$ nonzero summands give an $O(|m|/N)$ bound. The same holds with $\psi^2$ and for any residue subset. Pushforward contracts total variation, so <a href="#eq:summed-stationary-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:summed-stationary-mass">[eq:summed-stationary-mass]</a> and <a href="#eq:rounding-mass-removal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rounding-mass-removal">[eq:rounding-mass-removal]</a> allow us to replace the retained squared coefficient measures by $N^{-1}W(j/N,a)\psi(a)^2\,\mathrm da$, at total error $o(1)$, before pushing to temporal frequency. In particular, the actual lattice $j/N$ is never translated by $m\Delta_N$.

On the retained arc, the change of variable $y=sJ(a)$ gives <a id="eq:K-jacobian"></a>$$\label{eq:K-jacobian}
 K(s,y)=\frac{W(s,a)\psi(a)^2}{|s|J'(a)},
 \qquad a=J|_{I'}^{-1}(y/s),$$ with zero extension. The compact support of $\psi$ inside $I'$ makes this extension continuous (in fact smooth away from $s=0$). The real temporal frequency of a new angular index $j$ is <a id="eq:output-frequency"></a>$$\label{eq:output-frequency}
 t=j\sigma_N(a)=j/N+(j/N)J(a)/N.$$ The density of the pushforward of $N^{-1}W(j/N,a)\psi(a)^2\,\mathrm da$ under this map is exactly $K(j/N,Nt-j)$. The factor $N$ in the density change of variable cancels the coefficient mass $N^{-1}$.

Choose $Y$ so that $K(s,y)=0$ for $|y|>Y$. For fixed $t$, at most $2Y+2$ integers contribute to either $\sum_j K(j/N,Nt-j)$ or $\sum_jK(t,Nt-j)$; on every contributing term $|j/N-t|\le Y/N$. Uniform continuity of $K$, and a fixed bounded interval containing the $t$ supports for large $N$, prove that replacing $j/N$ by $t$ costs $o(1)$ in $L^1(\mathbb R)$. The same proof works after restriction to $j\equiv h\pmod P$. This is <a href="#eq:fine-density-unfolded" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fine-density-unfolded">[eq:fine-density-unfolded]</a>. Folding modulo one contracts the $L^1$ norm and finishes the proof. ◻

</details>
