Uniform convergence, compactness, and $S_nS_n^{-1}=S_n^{-1}S_n=\operatorname{id}$ give $TR=RT=\operatorname{id}$, so $T$ is a smooth diffeomorphism with smooth inverse. For every continuous $g$, $$\int g\circ T=\lim_n\int g\circ S_n=\int g,$$ so normalized volume is invariant. The vectors $f_n$ converge in $L^2$ to a real centered vector $f$.

<a id="eq:limit-orthonormal"></a>
Every completed stage has density exactly one. Combining <a href="#eq:strong-koopman" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:strong-koopman">[eq:strong-koopman]</a> with $f_n\to f$, for all $r,s\in\mathbb Z$ we obtain $$\label{eq:limit-orthonormal}
 \langle U_T^rf,U_T^sf\rangle
 =\lim_n\langle U_{S_n}^rf_n,U_{S_n}^sf_n\rangle
 =\delta_{rs}.$$ The retained finite polynomials give, for every $j$, $$\|h_j-P_j(U_T)f\|_2
 \le |\mathbb T\setminus I_{j-1}|^{1/2}+2^{-j}
       +\sum_{n>j}2^{-n}
 =|\mathbb T\setminus I_{j-1}|^{1/2}+2^{1-j}.$$ Along the infinitely many occurrences of any fixed test the right side tends to zero. Hence every test belongs to $\mathcal C(U_T,f)$. Their complex linear span is dense in complex $L^2_0(\mathbb T^3)$, whereas every orbit vector is centered. Thus $$\mathcal C(U_T,f)=L^2_0(\mathbb T^3).$$ Together with <a href="#eq:limit-orthonormal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-orthonormal">[eq:limit-orthonormal]</a>, this says that the bilateral orbit of $f$ is an orthonormal basis of the entire centered space. With $w=\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(z)$, the assignment $U_T^nf\mapsto w^n$ extends to a unitary map onto $L^2(S^1,m)$ intertwining $U_T$ with multiplication by $w$.

Multiplication by $w$ has no nonzero invariant vector, since $(w-1)g(w)=0$ forces $g=0$ almost everywhere. Consequently every $T$-invariant $L^2$ function is constant, which proves ergodicity. The manifold $\mathbb T^3$ is compact, connected, smooth, and without boundary, and its normalized flat volume has strictly positive smooth density. These are all the properties required in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:consequences"></a>
### Dynamical consequences

The spectral model gives ordinary mixing directly. The passage to higher orders uses the separate multiple-mixing theorem; the entropy and Lyapunov conclusions do not depend on that additional input.

<a id="stmt-cor-dynamical-profile"></a>
<div id="cor:dynamical-profile" class="corollary">

**Corollary 13**. *For the diffeomorphism $T$ and normalized standard volume $\mu$ of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, every integer $k\ge2$ and every fixed choice of measurable sets $A_1,\ldots,A_k\subseteq\mathbb T^3$ satisfy $$\mu\left(\bigcap_{i=1}^k T^{-t_i}A_i\right)
 \longrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^k\mu(A_i)$$ whenever $t_1<\cdots<t_k$ are integers and $\min_{1\le i<k}(t_{i+1}-t_i)\to\infty$. Moreover, the Kolmogorov–Sinai entropy satisfies $h_\mu(T)=0$, and all three Lyapunov exponents of $T$, counted with multiplicity, vanish at $\mu$-almost every point.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-dynamical-profile"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-dynamical-profile</summary>

*Proof.* Let $V:L^2_0(\mathbb T^3,\mu)\to L^2(S^1,m)$ be the unitary spectral model in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. For $a,b\in L^2_0(\mathbb T^3,\mu)$, $$\int_{\mathbb T^3}(a\circ T^n)\overline b\,\mathrm d\mu
 =\int_{S^1}w^n(Va)(w)\overline{(Vb)(w)}\,\mathrm dm(w)
 \longrightarrow0\qquad(|n|\to\infty).$$ Indeed, $(Va)\overline{Vb}\in L^1(m)$ by Cauchy–Schwarz, so the limit is the Riemann–Lebesgue lemma. Taking centered indicators gives ordinary mixing. The map $T$ is an invertible probability-preserving transformation with measurable inverse, so the multiple-mixing theorem (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[MultipleMixing2026](#ref-MultipleMixing2026)] gives every order $k\ge3$. Invariance shifts $t_1$ to zero and identifies its positive time gaps with $t_{i+1}-t_i$; the case $k=2$ is ordinary mixing.

The full Koopman operator is the direct sum of the one-dimensional constants and the simple Lebesgue model on all of $L^2_0$, and hence has finite spectral multiplicity. Normalized torus volume is a Lebesgue probability space, and $T$ is a measure-preserving automorphism. Rokhlin’s finite-multiplicity entropy theorem (Rokhlin 1960, sec. 7, p. 13) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Rokhlin1960](#ref-Rokhlin1960)] therefore gives $h_\mu(T)=0$.

The derivative and inverse derivative are bounded on the compact torus, so the invertible Oseledets integrability conditions hold. Write $\lambda_1(x),\lambda_2(x),\lambda_3(x)$ for the exponents repeated according to multiplicity at regular points. Pesin’s entropy formula, as stated and reproved by Mañé (Mañé 1981, 95, equation (2)) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Mane1981](#ref-Mane1981)], applies to the $C^\infty$ diffeomorphism and its absolutely continuous invariant probability $\mu$. Thus $$0=h_\mu(T)=\int_{\mathbb T^3}\sum_{i=1}^3\max\{\lambda_i(x),0\}\,\mathrm d\mu(x).$$ The nonnegative integrand vanishes almost everywhere, so every exponent is nonpositive there. Preservation of standard volume gives $|\det DT_x|=1$, and at every Oseledets-regular point $$\sum_{i=1}^3\lambda_i(x)
 =\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac1n\log|\det D(T^n)_x|=0.$$ The three nonpositive exponents must therefore all be zero at $\mu$-almost every point. ◻

</details>

{

      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "Mixing of every order for the constructed map follows from the multiple-mixing theorem applied to an invertible mixing transformation.",
      "relation": "uses",
      "source_anchor": "proof-stmt-cor-dynamical-profile",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-144.1",
      "source_quote": "so the multiple-mixing theorem (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1)",
      "target_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-145.1",
      "target_quote": "Every invertible mixing probability-preserving transformation is mixing of every finite order.",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The smooth transformation with simple Lebesgue spectrum is strongly mixing and therefore specializes the class of mixing transformations for which multiple ergodic averages converge pointwise almost everywhere.",
      "relation": "specialises",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-144.1",
      "source_quote": "Consequently $T$ is ergodic, and $U_T$ on this space is unitarily equivalent to multiplication by the coordinate $w$ on $L^2(S^1,m)$.",
      "target_anchor": "thm:main",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-154.1",
      "target_quote": "Suppose that $T$ satisfies [eq:mixing].",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "inferred",

      "explanation": "The constructed diffeomorphism with simple Lebesgue spectrum is mixing and therefore specializes the class of measure preserving transformations where fourfold ergodic averages converge almost everywhere.",
      "relation": "specialises",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-144.1",
      "source_quote": "Consequently $T$ is ergodic, and $U_T$ on this space is unitarily equivalent to multiplication by the coordinate $w$ on $L^2(S^1,m)$.",
      "target_anchor": "thm:main",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-154.2",
      "target_quote": "be invertible, bimeasurable, measure preserving, and mixing in the sense of [eq:mixing].",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The smooth mixing diffeomorphism provides a concrete smooth system specializing the mixing transformations on which nonlinearly scheduled triple ergodic averages converge almost everywhere.",
      "relation": "specialises",
      "source_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-144.1",
      "source_quote": "Consequently $T$ is ergodic, and $U_T$ on this space is unitarily equivalent to multiplication by the coordinate $w$ on $L^2(S^1,m)$.",
      "target_anchor": "thm:main",
      "target_pm_id": "PM-154.3",
      "target_quote": "For every system satisfying [eq:mixing], every triple of pairwise distinct nonzero integers",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },

      "basis": "inferred",
      "direction": "directed",
      "explanation": "The smooth torus diffeomorphism with simple Lebesgue spectrum is mixing and therefore specializes the general setting of invertible mixing transformations where triple ergodic averages converge pointwise.",

      "source_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
      "source_pm_id": "PM-144.1",
